(the recap's line) with the unlocks kept when there were any; the report's `ended` never read, so in the minute before the sweep its figures, counted to the bell, show as any session's; read once as the card shows, again only by Try again; Loading the last session… and Couldn't load the last session. with Try again; Open the recap to the class page once read; none until a class's first session ends (PV1), and none while the last read counts a session running, so from a bell or an End elsewhere until the account's read answers the rail holds the block's card alone, never the session before it (`docs/DECISIONS.md`, 2026-10-16)

### Phase 7 steps (live lesson; one PR each; 👀 = the owner looks at screenshots, 📱 = needs the owner's iPhone, 🔧 = the owner in a console)

The finished steps' full lines; the open steps are in `docs/PLAN.md`. The section's intro as it read on 2026-10-13:

Planned 2026-10-05 (`docs/DECISIONS.md`); the design is ARCHITECTURE's "Live lesson (Phase 7)",
and every step builds against it. **Status: building — the owner lifted the hold and approved both canvases on 2026-10-10.** The steps with no screen come first: M1 (its PR writes M0's runbook, so it goes ahead), then the backend steps (L1–L4 with L3b, W1, then M2–M4), then the logic with no UI (L5, L6, W2, M7, M9). **No screen was to be built until M0 was done** (the owner's order, 2026-10-10; L0b was in it until the owner moved it to before the first actual pilot, 2026-10-12), and M0 is done (2026-10-12); **the web screens come after the portal redesign's PV15** (the owner, 2026-10-12: its groundwork, its side menu and the pages they sit on first; "Portal redesign (v2's look) steps"). Then the UI, **web first** (the owner, 2026-10-10): in each slice the teacher's portal, then the student web, then the phone (Slice 1: L7, W3, W4, L7b, L3c, L7c, then L8; Slice 2: M5, M6, W5, then M8); the redesign's PV16 and PV17 follow the web screens, not waiting for the phone's (L8, M8). Each step is one PR from a fresh worker, under ~400 changed
lines not counting tests; a step that grows past that is split (L1a, L1b…). **Cloud-buildable:**
L1–L6 (L3b and L3c included), M1–M4, M7, M9, W1, W2. **The Mac session's:** the UI steps, L7, L7b, L7c, L8, M5,
M6, M8, W3, W4, W5, each built to its canvas: the
[app screens](https://claude.ai/artifact/CZpFbFuxnRrLcuywtJGfVQ) (L8, M8; approved 2026-10-10) and
[Bali portal in v2's look](https://claude.ai/artifact/PxWUcuMvv2CGngMqAtqvq4) (L7, L7b, L7c, M5, M6, W3–W5; approved
2026-10-12), which replaced the look of the [portal screens](https://claude.ai/artifact/BxHbGud1xMeyXLUUvsewHM)
of 2026-10-10, their words and states carried over except where it changes them (ARCHITECTURE,
"Live lesson", decisions 11–13; DESIGN.md, Sources). The exit checks, M0 and L0b are
the owner's.

Slice 1 — live questions:
- **L0** This docs PR: ARCHITECTURE's "Live lesson (Phase 7)", these steps, the decision entry — validation: docs only; merged as the plan, the build on hold
- **L1** Database and engine: `questions` and `responses` (drizzle-kit migration, the partial unique index on the open question); open, close (with reveal) and answer transitions, the open closing any open question and every session end (End, the sweep, a Start past the bell) closing it, `question_opened` and `question_closed` through `insertEvent`, an answer writing no event, a later answer replacing an earlier only when its `event_id` sorts after; each new key to `users` placed in the three coverage guards, each entry true to the code at that commit (the student export's responses included, since the guard fails without them; L2 rewrites the entries whose handling it changes); the engine's `event_id` check reads `responses` too, so no tap, unlock or other mutation spends an id an answer holds (settled 2026-10-10) — validation: PGlite transition tests, and real-Postgres races: answer vs close, answer vs session end, two opens at once (the open-question index's violation retried so the later open closes the earlier, a `questions.event_id` violation answered as a replay, neither a `500`); `sessionReport` and the grid ignore the new types; a tap reusing an answer's `eventId` is `409 event_id_conflict` — ✅ (#310) migration 0021: `questions` (its partial unique index `questions_one_open_per_session`, and `questions_session_opened_idx` on `(session_id, opened_at)` for a session's questions in the order asked: L4's poll, L3's results, the recap) and `responses` (unique per student per question, and `responses_student_idx` for a student's answers: the export, L2's reads), each `event_id` unique; the two plain indexes added on the owner's ruling (2026-10-11), so L2–L4 add none for these reads. In `transitions.ts`, `openQuestion`, `closeQuestion` and `answerQuestion` for L3 and L4 to call. An open takes the session FOR UPDATE and closes the open one (`closed_by: newer_question`); a close takes the session FOR SHARE and the question FOR UPDATE; `endHeldSession` (End, the sweep, A18, a disposal) closes the open one at the session's end, the bell for the sweep and A18 (`session_end`); an answer takes both FOR SHARE, writes no event, and replaces the held one only when its `eventId` sorts after. A unique violation reruns the transaction once, so neither backstop is a `500`; the open-question index's is unreachable through the engine (a question's insert takes its key-share lock on the session, behind the open's FOR UPDATE), so its race stages a raw insert with foreign-key triggers off. Refusals the API has throw (`SESSION_NOT_FOUND`, `SESSION_NOT_RUNNING`, `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`); the new ones are outcomes, `question_not_found`, `question_closed`, `invalid_option`, for L3 and L4 to map to their reasons. `insertEvent` and `armTap` refuse an id an answer holds, an answer one any event or other response holds; an answer reads `responses` once, so its own duplicate committed meanwhile meets the row at the upsert and is the replay (santa's round-1 blocker, fixed on the owner's ruling, its race staged); `question_closed`'s `closed_by` is `QUESTION_CLOSED_BY` in `@bali/shared`. The export carries each response with its question's prompt, options and session; the guards place `questions.created_by` and `responses.student_id` as kept, rows L2 rewrites. Tests: `packages/db/test/questions.test.ts`, `races.test.ts`
- **L2** Privacy plumbing: account deletion keeps responses under the de-identified user (C3), the export shows each response with its question (C5), the disposal (C6a) and the retention run (C6b) empty question texts as decided, and the retention run reads a response answered after the year end as a record after the year (its student kept named and continuing, as `armed_taps`/`teacher_invites` already are; `responses.student_id`'s `RETENTION_COVERAGE` entry says so); `docs/RUNBOOKS.md`'s steps updated; decision 9's documents: the app's privacy manifest (`ios/Bali/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`, answers as Other User Content, C1's lint kept green) and `docs/APP-STORE.md`'s privacy table (answers as "Other User Content") — validation: the coverage tests, C3/C5/C6a/C6b tests with responses and questions present (C6b: an enrolled student whose only record after the year end is a response stays named and enrolled), a real-Postgres race of an answer against the account's deletion — ✅ (#311, 2026-10-11) in `transitions.ts`: the disposal empties the prompt and option texts of every question in the school's lessons, read by a subquery so its parameters never grow with the school (santa's round-1 blocker, fixed on the owner's ruling: an id list bound one parameter per lesson, and postgres.js refuses 65,534 or more), the retention run those of each teacher it de-identifies (`emptyQuestions`: each keeps its count of options and its correct option); the retention run reads an answer on or after the year's end as a record after the year (`activeSince`); the account deletion leaves a deleted teacher's questions, as it leaves their removed classes' names (one decision for all of a teacher's words at their own deletion, to make with M4: #313); every answer stays under the account each de-identifies, and a deleted account's export keeps them (C5 carried them since L1). `answerQuestion` takes the student's row FOR SHARE first and refuses an answer reaching a deleted account `ACCOUNT_DELETED`, nothing recorded: L4's route gets `409 account_deleted` from the API's engine-code map. The three coverage maps' changed entries rewritten; RUNBOOKS 11 and 12 say what happens to questions and answers; `docs/APP-STORE.md`'s Other User Content row names answers, and the manifest needed no change (that type was already declared, linked, App Functionality, not tracking). Tests: `account-deletion.test.ts`, `student-record.test.ts`, `school-disposal.test.ts` (the emptying's statement binding no id per lesson among them), `retention.test.ts`, and in `races.test.ts` an answer staged against the deletion and against the retention run, both ways. Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-11, L2)
- **L3** Teacher API, split by its size into L3a and L3r (the planner's split, decided in advance; L3a alone is ~400 changed lines):
- **L3a** Open and close: `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/questions`, `POST /v1/questions/{id}/close`, their validation and refusals, the boot snapshot's open question; the new reasons in `@bali/shared`; S1 matrix rows, OpenAPI snapshot — validation: integration tests (happy path, authz, validation incl. a `correctOption` out of range, idempotent replay, a close after the bell answering closed), `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test` — ✅ (#312) `apps/api/src/routes/questions.ts`: both answer `QuestionResponse` (`outcome` `opened`, `closed` or `replay`; the question as `TeacherQuestion`: id, prompt, options, `correctOption`, `revealed`, `open`), `open` by `questionOpen` (`transitions.ts`: never closed, and its session running by the server's clock), for which the engine's question results now carry the question's own session; so a close past the bell, before the sweep, answers `replay` with `open: false`, and the snapshot's `question` is the open one by that clock, else null (`findUnclosedQuestion`, `teacherQuestion`). The body is checked whole (the engine stores what it is given): `eventId` a UUIDv7 (`z.uuidv7()`), the prompt and each option trimmed, plain text (the display name's `UNPRINTABLE`, line breaks in the prompt only), something visible, at most 500 and 200 code points, 2–6 options (`QUESTION_*` in `@bali/shared`), `correctOption` an index among them. The open is `requireSessionOwner`'s; the close's guard, `requireOwnQuestion` (`findQuestion`), is a student's and another teacher's `403` and an unknown question's `404 question_not_found`. `question_not_found`, `question_closed` and `invalid_option` join `API_ERROR_REASONS` and BaliCore's `ApiErrorReason`; contract-fixtures leaves the three out of the phone's refusals until L4's answer route gives them fixtures. Tests: `apps/api/test/questions.test.ts`. Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-11, L3a)
- **L3r** Results: `GET /v1/questions/{id}/results` over decision 3's one population, read through one guarded count in `packages/db` (one statement, so N never exceeds M; `counts` null under 3 answers; no answer's time and no student id leaves it) that L7c's recap and any report read too; a strong ETag over the body and `If-None-Match` → `304`, small and reusable for L4's presentation read, with `etag` in CORS's exposed headers so the portal's poll can read it; reuses L3a's `requireOwnQuestion` and `teacherQuestion`'s `open`; S1 rows, OpenAPI — validation: integration tests (the guard at 2 and 3 answers; its one population: an answer from a student removed mid-session still counted, N never above M; `open` false past the bell; `304` while nothing changed, `200` and a new ETag once an answer lands; authz), `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test` — ✅ (#314) `questionCounts(db, questionId)` (`packages/db/src/queries.ts`) is the one guarded count: one statement, answering `{ answered, eligible, counts }` and nothing else of an answer; L7c's recap calls it per question. `sendTagged` (`apps/api/src/etag.ts`) is the ETag: it sends the body as JSON under a strong tag, the sha256 of those bytes, and answers `304` to an `If-None-Match` holding it (in a list, weak, or `*`); L4's presentation read calls it as is. Every `/v1` answer stays `no-store`, so a poller sends `If-None-Match` itself: CORS allows it and exposes `etag`. The body is `QuestionResults` in `@bali/shared`. Tests: `apps/api/test/questions.test.ts`, `cors.test.ts`, and a real-Postgres race, counts read while answers land (`packages/db/test/races.test.ts`). *Amended 2026-10-12 (the owner's ruling on #315):* M counts every student in the class at any time while the question could be answered (an enrollment from before its close, any while it is open, not ended before its opening), beside the participants and N's, so a removal never changes a question's M; one condition in `questionCounts`. Tests: `apps/api/test/questions.test.ts` (a removal while open and once closed, answered or not; a join while open counted, one after the close and a removal before the opening not). Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-12, the owner's rulings on Phase 7's open items)
- **L3b** Saved questions (ARCHITECTURE, "Live lesson", decision 12): `saved_questions` (drizzle-kit migration), the engine's writes idempotent on `event_id`, the list, create, replace and remove routes for the teacher's own drafts, `saved_question_not_found` in `@bali/shared`; the account deletion removes a teacher's drafts, the disposal and the retention run empty their texts, the coverage guards placed; `saved_questions` joins the engine's `event_id` check both ways (settled 2026-10-10); S1 rows, OpenAPI — validation: PGlite transition tests, integration tests (happy path, authz, validation, idempotent replay, an `eventId` a tap or an answer holds refused), the coverage tests. Split by its size into L3b1 and L3b2 (the split decided in advance; the whole is ~650 changed lines):
- **L3b1** The data, the engine and the privacy paths: `saved_questions` (drizzle-kit migration), the engine's writes, `event_id` both ways, the account deletion, the disposal and the retention run, the coverage guards — validation: PGlite transition tests, real-Postgres races, the coverage tests, a tap, an unlock and an open refusing a draft's `eventId` through the API — ✅ (#316) migration 0022: `saved_questions` (`saved_questions_teacher_idx` on `teacher_id`; `updated_at` is the teacher's last write, so the page's "Edited"). In `transitions.ts`, `createSavedQuestion`, `replaceSavedQuestion` and `removeSavedQuestion` for L3b2's routes, each answering `{ outcome: 'created' | 'replaced' | 'removed' | 'replay', savedQuestion }` or `{ outcome: 'saved_question_not_found' }` (a removed draft, or none): the route checks the draft is the caller's first, as L3a's close does. A create's retry once its draft is removed is never a `404` (corrected by L3b2, on Claude Review's note on #316): the teacher's removal took the draft's id, so the retry writes the draft anew; a draft a deletion, a disposal or a retention run removed keeps the create's id, which the engine answers `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT` (through the route the caller is no teacher by then: `403`). A create holds the teacher's row FOR SHARE (`ACCOUNT_DELETED` for an account deleted on its way); a replace and a removal lock the draft's row alone. A replay is the same write again (a create: a live draft of the caller's with these words; a replace: the draft live with these words; a removal: the draft removed); the id the draft holds from another write is `EVENT_ID_CONFLICT`, as is one an event, an answer or another draft holds; a draft keeps only its last write's id; L3b2 gave a replace an answer's order (decision 4), so L7b need not send a draft's saves one at a time. Every other writer refuses a draft's id (`idHeldBy`: answers and drafts in one statement, so a tap pays no more than before). The account deletion, the disposal and the retention run remove a teacher's drafts, their words emptied; a draft written after the year's end keeps its teacher named. Tests: `packages/db/test/saved-questions.test.ts`, `account-deletion.test.ts`, `school-disposal.test.ts`, `retention.test.ts`, `races.test.ts`, `apps/api/test/questions.test.ts`. Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-11, L3b1)
- **L3b2** The routes: `GET /v1/saved-questions` (never a removed one, newest first by `created_at`), `POST /v1/saved-questions`, `PUT /v1/saved-questions/{id}`, `DELETE /v1/saved-questions/{id}` `{ eventId }`, the teacher's own drafts only (a student and another teacher's draft `403`; a removed or unknown draft `404 saved_question_not_found`, additive in `@bali/shared`, BaliCore's mirror and the reason tests); bodies validated as L3a's open (its schema reused); `PUT` joins CORS's methods (the portal calls it; `app.ts` and `cors.test.ts` leave it out today); S1 matrix rows, the OpenAPI snapshot — validation: integration tests (happy path, authz, validation, idempotent replay, an `eventId` a tap or an answer holds refused), `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test` — ✅ (#318) in `apps/api/src/routes/questions.ts`: each write answers `SavedQuestionResponse` (`outcome` `created`, `replaced`, `removed` or `replay`; the draft as `SavedQuestion`: id, prompt, options, `correctOption`, `eventId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, "Edited" once `updatedAt` is later), the list `SavedQuestionsResponse` (`listSavedQuestions`: newest first by `created_at`, then id). A write's guard, `requireOwnSavedQuestion` (`findSavedQuestion`, a removed draft too, for its removal's retry), is a student's and another teacher's `403` and an unknown draft's `404 saved_question_not_found`, as is the engine's `saved_question_not_found`; a save's body is L3a's open schema itself. The engine's replace applies only when its `eventId` sorts after the draft's (decision 4's order; the conductor's ruling on L3b1's WARN): an older save landing late answers `replay` with the draft now, and the draft carries its `eventId`, so L7b mints its next save after it; a removal stays unordered, and final. CORS allows `PUT`. Tests: `apps/api/test/questions.test.ts` (two saves landing out of order among them), `cors.test.ts`, `authorization-matrix.test.ts`, `packages/db/test/saved-questions.test.ts`, `races.test.ts` (the two saves staged both ways), `account-deletion.test.ts`. Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-11, L3b2)
- **L3c** A session's questions for its report, before L7c (v2's canvas draws L7c's Questions on a session's report page, `ReportOpened`, not on the class page's recap): a read of the questions asked in a session, in the order asked, each with its words and its totals through L3r's one guarded count (`questionCounts`: N of M, `counts` null under 3 answers); the class's own teacher only; S1 rows, the OpenAPI snapshot — validation: integration tests (the guard at 2 and 3 answers, the order asked, a session with none, validation of a malformed id, authz), `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test` — ✅ (#391) `GET /v1/classes/{id}/reports/sessions/{sessionId}/questions` (`apps/api/src/routes/reports.ts`), beside R2's report and refused by R2's own guard, now shared (`reportedSession`): another teacher and a student `403`, an unknown class `404 class_not_found`, a session not in the class `404 session_not_found`, a malformed id `400`. It answers `SessionReportQuestionsResponse` (`@bali/shared`): `questions`, each a `SessionReportQuestion`, the question as `TeacherQuestion` gives it (id, prompt, options, `correctOption`, `revealed`, `open` by the server's clock), `openedAt`, and its totals, `answered`, `eligible` and `counts`, read through `questionCounts` one question at a time, so each is the results route's, a question still open included; no student, no answer's time. In the order asked: `sessionQuestions` (`packages/db/src/queries.ts`) orders a session's questions by their opens' `question_opened` events' `seq`, never by `opened_at` nor by the order closed, which racing routes' clocks can swap. No ETag: the report page reads it once. For L7c: "2 asked in this session" is the list's length, "21 of 28 answered" `answered` of `eligible`, "Too few answers to show a breakdown" `counts` null, "Answer shown" `revealed`, the time `openedAt`. Tests: `apps/api/test/reports.test.ts` (the guard at 2 and 3 answers against the results route's own answer, open and past the bell; the order asked under two staged races; a session with none; nothing named; who may read it), `authorization-matrix.test.ts`, and on real Postgres two opens racing, the later time sent first every other round (`packages/db/test/races.test.ts`). Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-15, L3c)
- **L4** Student API: `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/presentation` (ETag/`304`, the correct option hidden until revealed, the caller's own answer with its `eventId`), `POST /v1/questions/{id}/answers` (`409 event_id_conflict` for an `eventId` held by any other response, the caller's own on another question included, or by any `events` row; a `responses.event_id` unique violation re-read, never a `500`); fixtures for each outcome; the demo asks and answers; the load gate runs several classes of 30 behind one school's address, each student polling every 2 s from a phone and a laptop (settled 2026-10-10) — validation: integration tests, `npm run fixtures` drift check, `npm run demo`, the load gate with zero `429`s for the polling classes (a dedicated budget only if it fails). Split by its size into L4a and L4b (the split decided in advance; the whole is ~800 changed lines by santa's count, ~450 of them generated JSON):
- **L4a** The routes: both, their validation and refusals, S1 rows, the OpenAPI snapshot, the fixtures, and the phone-fixture check no longer leaving out `question_not_found`, `question_closed` and `invalid_option` — validation: integration tests (happy path, authz, validation, idempotent replay, the conflict cases, the `304`), `npm run fixtures` drift check, `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test` — ✅ (#320) in `apps/api/src/routes/questions.ts`: the poll answers `Presentation` (`running`, by `sessionRunning`; `question`, a `StudentQuestion`: id, prompt, options, `open`, `correctOption` only once revealed, and the caller's own `answer` with its `eventId`), the open question, else the one closed last (`findShownQuestion`), none once the session is over, under L3r's `sendTagged`; an answer, through `answerQuestion`, answers `AnswerResponse` (`outcome` `answered` or `replay`, and the `answer` now). Both are for a student enrolled in the session's class now (`findEnrolledStudent`, which creates no account), anyone else `403`; an unknown session is `404 session_not_found`, an unknown question `404 question_not_found`; a malformed answer `400 invalid_request`, an option the question lacks `400 invalid_option`, past the close or the bell `409 question_closed`, a spent id `409 event_id_conflict`, a deleted account `409 account_deleted`. Thirteen fixtures (`contracts/fixtures/presentation/`, `answers/`), and BaliCore's wire types and two plain calls, `presentation(session:)` and `answer(question:_:)`, with them: CI's Swift job decodes every fixture and wants a call for each (moved from L5). Tests: `apps/api/test/student-lesson.test.ts`, `authorization-matrix.test.ts`, `contract-fixtures.test.ts`; BaliCore's `ContractFixtureTests`, `APIClientTests`, `VocabularyTests`. Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-11, L4a)
- **L4b** The demo asks a question and its students answer it (`env -u DEMO_API_URL npm run demo`); the load gate's polling classes: several classes of 30 behind one school's address, each student polling `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/presentation` every 2 s from a phone and a laptop (settled 2026-10-10) — validation: the demo, `npm run load:gate` with zero `429`s for the polling classes (a dedicated budget only if it fails, and why in DECISIONS) — ✅ (#321) the demo's 8:12am incident (`apps/api/scripts/demo.ts`): the teacher opens a question in the running session, Ana's poll shows it with no correct option, Ana, Ben and Dana answer, the results say 1 of 4 with `counts` null and then 3 of 4 with them, the close reveals the answer, a late answer is `409 question_closed`, and the stream carries `question_opened` and `question_closed` while the log holds no answer's `eventId`. The gate (`apps/api/scripts/load/gate.js`): the first five bell classes (`LESSON_CLASSES`) follow a live lesson, each teacher asking a question after the Start (`kind:ask`), each student a phone and a laptop (`device()`) opening it within 30 s of the bell, finding the session in `/v1/me`'s `liveSession` and polling the presentation every 2 s with `If-None-Match` (`kind:poll`), the phone answering once (`kind:answer`); none of theirs refused, so no dedicated budget (60 of the account's 120 a minute). Opening within 10 s pushed the Starts' p95 to 867 ms on the database's 10 connections, so the window is a phone's 30 s read. M9 shows its slide to these same devices. Measured: no school request refused, a poll's p95 4.7 ms, an answer's 17.5, a Start's 329. `apps/api/test/student-lesson.test.ts` pins the question the poll shows: the open one, else the one closed last, the two racing opens' outcome staged so "opened last" fails it (L4a's WARN, the conductor's ruling). Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-11, L4b)
- **L5** BaliCore: a foreground poller (2 s, `If-None-Match`, the `304` keeping the last answer, stops when the session isn't running), the answer call's honest failure; the presentation and answer types and two plain calls landed with L4a's fixtures — validation: `swift test` on Linux in CI, the fixture-decoding tests — ✅ (#330) `LiveLesson` (`ios/BaliCore/Sources/BaliCore/LiveLesson.swift`), an actor per running session, for L8 to wire with no screen of its own: `run()` reads `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/presentation` every 2 s (`interval`) with the last `ETag` as `If-None-Match` (`APIClient.presentation(session:ifNoneMatch:)`; `APIResponse` now reads `etag` and a `429`'s `retryAfter`), a `304` keeping the last read and the student's answer in it, until a read says the session isn't running or its task is cancelled (the owner's: run while the app is in front, cancelled as it goes behind; a read cancelled on its way keeps nothing); a `429` holds it for its `Retry-After`, from 2 s to `longestWait`, 60 s, which none or a date waits. It keeps `state` (`LessonState`: `presentation`, and the last poll's `problem`, `PollProblem`: `unreachable`, `rateLimited`, or `refused` with its status and reason, the poll trying again in 2 s) and streams its changes, `updates`, the newest kept. `answer(question:option:)` sends one request through the client it was given, never the outbox, under an id minted after the answer held and after the last minted there (`EventID.mint(at:after:)`, moved to BaliCore from BaliOutbox: the millisecond past the held id's when the clock stands behind), and answers `AnswerResult`: `answered`, the answer the server holds, kept in `state` as the one whose id sorts last (so a read made before it landed never takes it back); `questionClosed`, `invalidOption`, `eventIdConflict`, `accountDeleted`, known by reason, never message; `unreachable`; or `refused` with its status and reason: each a line the approved canvas's card says, with a tap again. Tests: `ios/BaliCore/Tests/BaliCoreTests/LiveLessonTests.swift` (a scripted server and a clock that moves only as the poll waits: the cadence, the `304`, the stop, the `429`'s wait, the failures, the cancel, each refusal, the ids from a clock a minute behind), `APIClientTests` (a real `304` over a socket). - **L6** Portal logic in `apps/web/src/lib`: the question's state, opening and closing, the results poller (2 s, each tab while it is visible, stops when closed), no UI — validation: vitest unit tests, `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test` — ✅ (#332) `apps/web/src/lib/live-question.ts`, for L7 to wire, holding no words (L7 says each answer as the canvas does): `openQuestion(api, attempt)` and `closeQuestion(api, attempt)` answer `opened` or `closed` with the question now (`TeacherQuestion`, a replay's too, a question closed since included), or a `Failure`: `refused` with its `status` and `reason`, nothing changed (`session_not_running`: the session ended first; `event_id_conflict`; a `400`), or `failed`, no answer to go by (unreachable with no `status`, a timeout, a `5xx`, or a `429` with its `retryAfter`). `openAttemptFor` and `closeAttemptFor` mint a UUIDv7, the unanswered attempt reused for the same words or the same choice, and `keepUnanswered` (session-controls, now for any answer) keeps one only while `failed`, so Try again resends it under its id. `questionAfter(question, event)` applies the stream's `question_closed` (closed, revealed as its close was) and answers `'unknown'` for a `question_opened` this tab doesn't hold, whose words only the snapshot has. `pollResults(api, questionId, onChange)` reads `GET /v1/questions/{id}/results` one read at a time, 2 s after each answer, while the tab is visible (hidden, none; back in view it waits out what is left of the 2 s), with the kept ETag through `ApiClient.getTagged` (`If-None-Match`, null for a `304`, which keeps the counts), handing `ResultsState` (`results`, `final`, `problem`) to `onChange`; a `429` waits its `Retry-After`, 2 s to a minute (L5's rule); any other failure is the `problem`, read again in 2 s. It stops once the question is closed: at the first good read saying so begun after a read saw it closed or `closed()` said so (the stream's `question_closed`, or this tab's close answered), #314's WARN as the conductor ruled; `results.open` false is the bell's close before the stream carries it. For W2: `getTagged` and `failureOf`. Slice 2 — slides:
- **M0** 🔧 The owner: a private bucket per environment (AWS S3, settled 2026-10-10), its CORS for the portal's origins, an IAM identity for that bucket only, its keys as Railway secrets — the runbook: `docs/RUNBOOKS.md`, runbook 10 (written with M1), dev first — validation: the runbook's check, `railway ssh --service bali -- npm run --silent deck-bucket:check` (a presigned PUT and GET from dev, the bucket's refusals and CORS, the API's read and delete), ending `PASS` — ✅ (2026-10-12, both environments) the owner's: dev's bucket `bali-decks-dev-eshshah` and prod's `bali-decks-prod-eshshah`, the check `PASS` through `railway ssh` on `bali` and on `bali prod`; deck storage is on in both
- **M1** The storage interface: S3 adapter (presigned PUT and GET, delete) and an in-memory fake that CI and the demo use; M0's runbook (`docs/RUNBOOKS.md`, the owner's bucket, its CORS, its IAM user and the check) rides this PR — validation: unit tests against the fake, the adapter's request signing tested offline — ✅ (2026-10-10) `DeckStorage` (`apps/api/src/deck-storage.ts`), what M2–M4 call, on `app.deckStorage` (undefined while storage is off): `uploadUrl(key, bytes)`, a presigned PUT for a PDF of exactly that length (`content-length` and `content-type` signed, so S3 refuses another), and `downloadUrl(key)`, a presigned GET answered `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Content-Encoding: identity`, so the bytes come as stored and no client decodes a file a crafted upload labelled gzip (santa's round 1; the owner's ruling, 2026-10-10), both good 5 minutes; `read(key, maxBytes)`, the bytes as stored, `missing` or `too_large`, through the download URL with S3 asked for one byte past the cap, an answer still naming an encoding refused unread (`EncodedAnswer`), so no read outgrows the cap; `delete(key)`, fine for one already gone; a refusal throws `StorageError` with S3's code and status, never its words. S3 at `<bucket>.s3.<region>.amazonaws.com`, signed by Node's own crypto, no SDK (`apps/api/src/sigv4.ts`: the Cognito deleter's signer, moved, and `presignV4`). A missing object reads `missing` on S3's 404 and on its 403, since the key has no `s3:ListBucket`. `memoryDeckStorage()` for CI and the demo: its URLs on `deck-storage.invalid`, answered by its own `fetch` with S3's refusals (another length or type, another method or key, past 5 minutes); injected as `buildApp`'s `deckStorage`. Config: `DECK_BUCKET`, `DECK_BUCKET_REGION`, `DECK_BUCKET_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `DECK_BUCKET_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, all four or none, a dotted bucket name refused (`.railway/railway.ts` preserves them); unset, off, and the boot says `deck storage is off`. No route calls it yet (M2). M0's runbook: runbook 10, its check `npm run deck-bucket:check` (`apps/api/scripts/deck-bucket-check.ts`). Tests: `apps/api/test/sigv4.test.ts` (S3's published examples), `deck-storage.test.ts` (the AWS SDK signer's values for this API's PUT, GET and delete; a gzip-labelled object read and downloaded as stored, against a local S3 stand-in through Node's own fetch; the stand-in; the check against it; the boot line), `env.test.ts`. Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-10, M1)
- **M2** Deck endpoints: create (presigned PUT), complete (magic bytes, ≤ 25 MB, sha256, ≤ 200 pages, not encrypted; the parse in a separate worker under a hard time limit, settled 2026-10-10), list, delete; the sweep rejects a deck pending 24 hours; S1 rows, OpenAPI — validation: integration tests with real and hostile PDFs (encrypted, wrong magic, oversized, too many pages, one that stalls the parser past the limit while the API keeps answering), authz, idempotent replay. Split by its size into M2a and M2b (the split decided in advance; the whole is ~900 changed lines by santa's count):
- **M2a** The table and the teacher's routes: `decks` and its migration, the engine's writes, create, list and remove, the answers with storage off, S1 rows and OpenAPI for those three — validation: PGlite and real-Postgres tests, integration tests (authz, validation, idempotent replay, storage off) — ✅ (#323) migration 0023: `decks`, decision 4's columns and `event_id` (unique: the write that last wrote it, as a draft's), `decks_teacher_idx`, checks on `status` and a lowercase-hex `sha256`. In `transitions.ts`, `createDeck` (the school FOR KEY SHARE, then the teacher FOR SHARE, a class's create's order; `pending`, its key `decks/<id>.pdf`; a replay only while the deck is as the create left it) and `removeDeck` (the deck row alone, FOR UPDATE, so M3's `deck_in_use` reads the presentation rows there, unlocked); every other writer refuses an id a deck holds, and a deck's writes one an event, an answer, a draft or another deck holds. `apps/api/src/routes/decks.ts`: `POST /v1/decks` `{ eventId, title, bytes, sha256 }` answers `CreateDeckResponse`, the deck and its `uploadUrl` (a replay's a fresh one), more than `DECK_MAX_BYTES` (25 MiB) a `400` before any URL; `GET /v1/decks`, the caller's, newest first, none removed; `DELETE /v1/decks/{id}` `{ eventId }`, then the object deleted and `object_deleted_at` stamped (`stampObjectDeleted` in `queries.ts`, decision 5's exception), a `pending` deck's left unstamped since its upload URL may still write it, a failed delete logged and left unstamped. Storage off, every deck route answers a teacher `503 deck_storage_off`; `deck_not_found` and `deck_storage_off` join `API_ERROR_REASONS` and BaliCore's `ApiErrorReason`. The coverage guards place `decks.teacher_id` and `decks.school_id` as kept until M4. Ride-along (the owner's yes): `deck-storage.test.ts` pins an encoded answer's body unread. Tests: `packages/db/test/decks.test.ts`, `races.test.ts`; `apps/api/test/decks.test.ts`, `authorization-matrix.test.ts`. Why: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (2026-10-11, M2a)
- **M2b** The check: `POST /v1/decks/{id}/complete` `{ eventId }`, the object read by M1's capped `read` (the size cap first), then the `%PDF-` magic bytes, the size equal to the declared, the `sha256`, ≤ 200 pages and not encrypted, the parse in a separate worker (`node:worker_threads`) under a hard time limit; `ready` with its page count, or `rejected` with the reason, its object deleted after the commit as `DELETE` does it; a removed or rejected deck's object stamped `object_deleted_at`, and left for the sweep, only once 5 minutes have passed since its last upload URL was minted, so a re-upload after the check or a retried create racing a removal never leaves an object stamped gone (the conductor's ruling on M2a's review: M2a stamps by `status`, which holds only while no deck is checked); one-way, a replay answering the verdict; the engine's verdict write and its `event_id` both ways, as M2a's; the smallest PDF dependency that reads the page count and detects encryption (none is installed), and why in DECISIONS; the sweep rejects a deck pending 24 hours; S1 rows, OpenAPI — validation: integration tests with real and hostile PDFs (encrypted, wrong magic, oversized, too many pages, one that stalls the parser past the limit while the API keeps answering), authz, idempotent replay; `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test`. - **M2b1** Complete and the check: the route, M1's capped read, the magic, the size and the `sha256`, the parse in its worker under a hard time limit, the PDF dependency and why in DECISIONS, the hostile PDFs, S1 rows and OpenAPI; a rejected deck's object deleted after the commit as `DELETE` does it — validation: integration tests with real and hostile PDFs (encrypted, wrong magic, oversized, too many pages, one that stalls the parser past the limit while the API keeps answering), authz, idempotent replay — ✅ (#324) `POST /v1/decks/{id}/complete` `{ eventId }` (`apps/api/src/routes/decks.ts`): a pending deck is checked by `checkDeck` (`apps/api/src/deck-check.ts`: the read capped at `DECK_MAX_BYTES`, `%PDF-`, the length and WebCrypto's SHA-256, then `parsePdf`, `unpdf`'s PDF.js in a worker thread, `deck-check.worker.mjs`, stopped at 10 s or a 256 MB heap; two checks at a time in a process, `CHECKS_AT_ONCE`, the rest waiting their turn), outside any transaction, and the engine's `completeDeck` records the verdict under the deck's row lock, its `eventId` spent both ways; answered `200` with `outcome: 'checked'` and the deck `ready` with its pages or `rejected` with `rejection` (migration 0024; `DECK_REJECTIONS`: `not_pdf`, `too_large`, `mismatch`, `too_many_pages`, `encrypted`, `too_slow`, `unfinished`), each a line the approved "Your slides" design says; a deck checked already answers `replay` with its verdict, whatever the `eventId`. `DECK_MAX_PAGES` (200) joins the limits; `buildApp` takes a `parsePdf` (the tests' stalling parser); `findDeck` reads the whole row. - **M2b2** The 5-minute rule and the sweep: every upload URL's mint recorded on the deck (a column decision 4 gains, additive), a create's retry included (today it mints one and writes nothing), under the deck's row lock, through the engine; a removed or rejected deck's object deleted and stamped `object_deleted_at` only once that mint plus 5 minutes plus a margin for an upload in flight has passed (S3 checks a URL's expiry when an upload starts; the margin and why in DECISIONS), the sweep taking it before then by the same rule, so the complete's rejection, the removal and the sweep all follow it; the sweep rejects a deck pending 24 hours (`unfinished`), its object by the same rule; ARCHITECTURE decision 4's sentence on the API stamping right after the commit corrected, the new column listed; #323's WARN 3 left and recorded in DECISIONS (a create whose answer was lost, retried after its teacher removed that pending deck, makes a second pending deck with a fresh URL, which the sweep's 24 hours reject, as saved questions behave) — validation: PGlite and real-Postgres tests (a create's retry racing a removal), integration tests (the stamp waiting out the mint, the sweep's pass, the 24 hours), `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test` and `env -u DEMO_API_URL npm run demo` — ✅ (#325) migration 0025: `decks.upload_url_minted_at`, set by `createDeck` and by its replay under the deck's row lock (FOR UPDATE, after the school and the teacher). The rule, `dropObject` in `apps/api/src/routes/decks.ts`: a removed or rejected deck's object is deleted and stamped only once `OBJECT_WRITABLE_MS` has passed since that mint (the URL's 5 minutes and `UPLOAD_IN_FLIGHT_MS`, 25), at once by the route past it, else by the sweep. `sweepDecks`, the sweep's last duty while storage is on: `rejectUnfinishedDecks` (the engine; one guarded statement, `DECK_PENDING_LIMIT_MS`, 24 hours since the create, `unfinished`, the `eventId` kept), then `decksToDrop` (`queries.ts`: removed or rejected, unstamped, minted by the cutoff, the oldest first, `DROPS_PER_PASS` 50 together), a failed delete's and a crash's included. The poll's `slide` (`Slide`, null while nothing shows) and the snapshot's optional `presentation` (`SessionPresentation`: `slide` and the `eventId` of the slide change that set it, so a reloaded presenter, M6, mints its next after it; null before the first change and once over) both read it. `GET /v1/decks/{id}/download` (`apps/api/src/routes/decks.ts`) answers `DeckDownload` `{ url }`, M1's `downloadUrl` (5 minutes, `no-store`): a removed or unknown deck `404 deck_not_found` to anyone; its teacher, or a student whose class's running session shows it now (`sessionsShowingDeck` in `queries.ts`, then `sessionRunning`), else `403`; storage off `503 deck_storage_off` once the caller may see it; the teacher's own deck unchecked or rejected `409 deck_not_ready` / `deck_rejected`. Five fixtures (`presentation/slide`, `download/`: shown, 403, 404, 503), so `deck_not_found` and `deck_storage_off` are the phone's now; BaliCore's `Slide`, `DeckDownload` and `download(deck:)`, which M7 builds its cache on. - **M4** Privacy: decks in the account deletion (C3), the disposal and the retention run, titles emptied, stored objects deleted after the commit and the sweep's retry for any left unstamped (a deck's only once 30 minutes have passed since its last upload URL was minted, when none can write it again: M2b2's rule, which the sweep already applies to any removed deck), previews never touching storage, the coverage guards — validation: C3/C6a/C6b tests with decks present, the fake storage empty afterwards, a preview leaving it untouched, a failed delete retried by the sweep — ✅ (#329) `removeDecks` (`transitions.ts`): a teacher's account deletion, the disposal (the school's decks and its people's) and the retention run (a teacher it de-identifies) set each deck's `removed_at` (one removed already keeps its time) and empty its `title`, the presentation rows kept; none reaches the bucket, so a preview, rolled back, leaves it as it was, and the API's sweep deletes each object by M2b2's rule. A deck made after the school's year end is a record after the year (`activeSince`), as a draft is. A deck it holds asks nothing; else `download(deck:)` for a fresh URL, its bytes fetched through the client's transport (no redirect followed) with no bearer token, and a bucket `403`, as S3 answers an expired URL, asks for a fresh one once. The decks live in memory alone, never on disk: emptied once a read says the session is over, a fetch ending after that kept nowhere, and gone with the lesson when L8 drops it, as it must at sign-out; M8 renders with PDFKit and opens no link in a deck. Its fetch shares the transport's 30 s bound on a whole exchange (a `ponytail:` note): the case to try is a class fetching a big deck at once through one access point, several phones on school Wi-Fi, at M8's device check or M10's. Rode along, L5's follow-ups (#330's WARNs, the conductor's rulings): `run()` one at a time, the newest taking over and an older run stopping at its next read, saying nothing of it (a second call returning at once could leave none after a quick trip behind and back); a failed read after a read said the session is over sets no `problem`; and tests of two question ids and of an answer and a poll at once.
