- **F** Fixes from the owner's 2026-10-01 device check (#139–#151), one PR each — all proven on the owner's iPhone: #139–#141, #143, #145–#147 and #149–#151 at the 2026-10-02 re-test; #144, #166 and #167 at the final phone check (2026-10-03). F1 (#144) a running Bali notices Screen Time access taken back, with no relaunch — superseded by F1b below (its doubt retired; no re-test): Family Controls' read stays approved in a running app, so rule 3's check first runs a pass that asks iOS's DeviceActivity center whether it still holds the bell's window it took (`ScreenTime.holds`, `schedule(for:)` — read only, nothing stopped or replaced, so the monitor is not woken; not in a window's last minute, which iOS may end early). Gone, it is asked for again: refused as unauthorized (`ScreenTimeUnauthorized`), the permission is judged off at once; taken yet still not held, the approved read is judged not determined — B5a-2's grace, then off; held again — another app's grant changed, which ends every app's windows — nothing; refused otherwise, unscheduled as any refusal, no doubt. A window iOS takes and holds, or iOS's own prompt answered, ends the doubt. The off path is unchanged (protection off reported, the Screen Time off screen, its way on iOS's prompt, then a re-tap). Debug readout: `iOS:` (the read now, and the window iOS holds under each name, every 5 s) and `Found:` (a window gone, a window refused). Tests: `RevokedWhileRunningTests` (Linux and the simulator), red first. README round 1 step 12 rewritten for it. F2 (#145) Screen Time off within the grace after a launch ✅ (the 2026-10-02 re-test: off about 10 s after a launch with access off; never off over three relaunches with access on, in Low Power Mode and after a restart) — its grace kept by F1b only for a phone with no marker written yet, its not-begun guard retired: B5a-2's grace is one named constant, `Enforcer.grace`, 10 s (was a check-in interval, 30 s): the one sample — with access on, "Checking Screen Time…" about 2 s after a launch — times five. Within it, a check that reads Family Controls' own not determined is made again every second (`recheckAfter`, the enforcer's own timer, no engine wake), so the grace ends as iOS settles, or is judged off once it lasts; never over F1's doubt, where each check asks iOS's center for the window again. F1's review, its two WARNs: a doubt's end ends its run of not determined (`didSet`), and "not held" is a sign only once the bell's window has begun (`held`) — before, nothing is known of it, so no doubt starts or ends there; from santa's round 1, a check that did not report while its run is under way is always made again, so the report follows a claim of off. Debug readout: `Launch:` (how long the read was not determined after the launch) and `(not begun)` on `iOS:`. Tests: `LaunchGraceTests`, `RevokedWhileRunningTests` (F1's now in a class within its window), red first. README round 1 steps 12–13 say what the re-test must see. F3 (#140) Unlocked's reasons picked at once ✅: a pick shows its check and the light haptic at once and every reason stays open while a change of the reason is on its way (A20), where the others were off until it answered. `Phone.pick` keeps one change on its way and, once it answers, sends the newest pick unless it is the one just sent, the picks between never sent, so the server records them in the order the student made them and the grid and History show the last. A pick that did not go is said, the check back on the reason on record, and picked again it goes under its event id where no answer came (rule 4). A pick is for the unlock whose card it was made on (`UnlockedWords.unlock`): once the card is another's, the one waiting is dropped with its check, never sent to the next (santa's round 1). Before the unlock is sent, a pick still writes it into the record (C5a's hold). No server change. Tests: `AppTests.picksAtOnce`, `.picksNotSent`, `.picksForTheirUnlock`, `.picksOnThePhone`; `ReasonTests.changedLate` (a late answer for an unlock left behind keeps the next one's retry under its event id). F4 (#141) History shows at once ✅: the history read is kept while the app runs (`Phone.history`), forgotten only when who is signed in changes, so a visit shows its moments at once, never the title alone; its screen reads the newest page again from the top each time it shows (`refreshHistory`, on appear), quietly, that page taking the place of all read once it lands (`History.fromTop`: a read from the top replaces, Show earlier's page adds — older pages back under Show earlier) — and a sign-in reads it once, so even the first visit is instant. A read again that fails keeps the moments and says so above them, "Bali couldn't update your history." and why, with Try again (`History.notUpdated`, said through Show earlier's pages until a read from the top starts again or answers; fixture `historyRefreshError`); Show earlier's own failure stays where it was pressed. Paging unchanged. F6 (#146) a refused tap is said only while it is the phone's newest tap ✅ (the conductor's default, under the owner's delegation): Home, Waiting and Protection off said a scan of a block no teacher set up ("Bali doesn't know a block you tapped…", with Try again) for as long as it stayed stuck — on the owner's phone an hour on, in another class — since the card followed the latest stuck tap and an unknown block's never records. Now any later tap, scanned or typed, ends it (`refusedTapWords` checks `SyncState.lastTap`, the outbox file's own newest tap — `Outbox.lastTap`, kept by every tap since B6b — read as the engine starts — again with the queue while not known, should that read fail (santa's round 1) — and set as each tap is recorded, so a relaunch keeps it gone; a later refusal is said in its own words). The refused tap is kept, retried and listed in the readout's `Outbox:` line as before; no screen's choice and no unlock record changes. Not chosen (the owner's to pick): an end-of-day drop, a dismiss button. ARCHITECTURE's tap rule says so. Tests: `TapInTests.refusedTapThenTapped` (red on `main` first) and `.refusedTapUnreadAtLaunch`, `ScreenTests.refusedTapNewest` and the three wordings read with the file's newest tap, `ProtectionOffScreenTests.carried`; fixtures `homeTapRefused`, `homeTapRefusedThenTapped` (`AppTests.fixtures`, `.tabs`) F5 (#139) History scrolls without a shadow per line ✅ (smooth on the owner's phone, a Debug build, at the 2026-10-02 re-test): `Card` drew shadow-1 over the whole card, so SwiftUI shadowed every line, icon, chip, hairline and button inside it, each redrawn as a screen scrolls; the shadow is now the card's background fill's (`Theme.card.shadow(.drop(…))`), drawn once at its edge, D1's values unchanged. History's days and cards are a `LazyVStack`, only those on screen built, each card keyed by its own id (`History.Card.id`, its first moment's), never its position; F4's keeping and quiet refresh, paging and `screenWide()` unchanged. No visual change: all 66 fixtures (68 screens, the intro's pages each) shot by both builds seconds apart on the iPhone 17 simulator and pixel-diffed — outside the cards, the page and each card's own shadow identical, but 1–3 px where a full-width hairline inside a card meets its sides (≤ 3 of 255); inside, only the old per-element shadows gone (≤ 17 of 255) and History's unlock icon ⅓ pt over; the rest only the clock and the text caret. The fair re-test is a Release build on the phone (the stutter was seen in Debug, which has no optimisation), over a long history (weeks of moments), with Instruments' Animation Hitches before and after. Tests: `AppTests.cardShadow` and `.historyLazy` (red first), `HistoryTests.cardIds`. F7 (#151, #149) Waiting's Back to home goes to the regular Home, and Waiting fits ✅ (the owner's decision, 2026-10-01): Back to home opened Home over Waiting with a back arrow, no tab bar and "Tap your teacher's block", as if the student had not tapped. Now the router treats Home opened over Waiting as Home opened over Unlocked — tabbed, History and Me honoured (`Screen.choose`) — with no Back to Waiting (`Phone.canGoBack`), and Home's card in the hero's place says the wait (`SyncState.waitingCard`, C3c's look: a Waiting chip, "You're tapped in. Your phone locks when class starts, as long as Bali is open." and Tap in, secondary — only a Start or another tap ends the wait, so a wrong block or a Start that never comes is no dead end; the owner may drop it). Waiting stays the tap's answer; Bali opened again while waiting lands on it (the standing kept in the file, nothing opened kept); decision 6's read runs whatever the screen, and the Start is Focus. Waiting (#149) laid out as C4's Focus is: D1's two groups at their own heights, at least 24 pt apart, the cards 16 apart, the space above the ring giving first, so a second card — a failed read, a refused tap — fits the iPhone 17e, and the owner's iPhone 15 Pro, with Back to home; larger text scrolls. Tests: `ScreenTests.homeWaiting`, `.tabs`, `WaitingHomeTests.startFromHome` (red first); `AppTests.waitingFits` (red first: 782.7 pt in the 17e's 747; the 17e's and the 15 Pro's frames), `.startFromHomeWaiting`, `.opened`, `.wayBack`; fixtures `homeWaiting` (was `homeFromWaiting`) and `waitingTapRefused` (the owner's #149 case) F9 (#147) Me says whose sign-in this is ✅: under Sign out, "You're signed in as <email>." (`SignOutWords.signedIn`; a caption in `text-tertiary`, Sign out's helper as the name card's is, any failure under it; D1's Me otherwise unchanged, and Join's Sign out says none), so the name a student's teachers see is never taken for what they sign in with — on the owner's phone, a name changed to an email was expected to sign in. The email is the `email` claim of Cognito's ID token, read unverified from the token endpoint's answer at the sign-in and each renewal and kept in the Keychain record beside the tokens (`Tokens.email`, `SignIn.email()`; a renewal naming none keeps the same account's), read with who is signed in at each change (`Phone.follow`) and replaced then (`Phone.email`): none once signed out, and another student's sign-in shows theirs or none, never the last one's. Tokens a build before kept name none, so nothing is said until their first renewal (within the hour); no network call, no server change. Out of scope: changing the sign-in email from the app; a starting name from the account's `name`. Tests: `TokenTests.email` and `.earlierBuild` (red first), `MeTests.signedInAs`, `AppTests.signedInAs` and `.fixtures` (every `me*` fixture says Ana's email). F8 (#143) a student removed from their last class lands on Home, not the first-run Join ✅ (the owner's ask, 2026-10-01): with no class left, the router treated them as new (`settled`: out with no classes → Join), so a student removed from the roster mid-class landed on "Enter your class code", a Sign out link and no tab bar. Now a student this phone has seen in a class lands on Home, its tab bar, History and Me (one who leaves their last class on Me stays on Me, Home's card a tab away); a student never in a class here keeps Join, exactly as before. How it knows (`Phone.inClass`, kept as `everApproved` is): the id of the student `GET /v1/me` last listed in a class, in the app's own defaults (a fixture's never), compared with the id each read names (`everInClass`, the router's new input), so another student signing in on this phone never inherits it, a late read of the last one's classes included; kept across the same student's sign-out, as the engine keeps their classes then, and replaced once another student is listed in a class. Home's card in the hero's place (`SyncState.noClassesCard`; no D1 artboard, so C3c's look with no chip): "You're not in any classes. Join one with the class code from your teacher." and Join a class, primary, opened over Home with its way back (C3b); no Tap in there (a tap joins only a class the student is in) and no classes section, whose Join a class the card stands in for. `keepsOpened` closes what was opened, and the tab, at no classes only where Join is the router's own (`everInClass` its input too), so Join opened over Home, its code typed, and History or Me chosen there stay through a removal and every read after. Not here: saying what happened ("You're no longer in …"), the owner's to OK. Tests: `ScreenTests.inNoClass` (`removedFromLastClass` until 2026-10-07), `.keepsOpened` (red first); `AppTests.everInClass`, `.fixtures`, `.tabs`; fixture `homeNoClasses` F10 (#150) Join fades in, and Back lets its keyboard go first ✅: a screen the student opens over another — Join over Home or Me, Home over Waiting or Unlocked — fades in, and the one under it fades back in at Back: `Phone.open` and `Phone.back` move in `withAnimation(Theme.fade)`, DESIGN.md's `base` 200 ms on its `standard` easing, and nothing under Reduce Motion, where every animation is off. Only the student's own moves fade: the router's — Focus at the Start among them — and a join's answer closing Join stay instant. The screen arriving is drawn over the page's own colour from its first frame (`Opening`, in `RootView`), so the one it replaces is gone at once: SwiftUI keeps that one until the fade ends, and it draws itself anew meanwhile from what the phone says now — Home grew Join's Back arrow and slid 44 pt, seen on the simulator — so it leaves unseen, with no animation, and the arriving screen's colour covers it too, whichever of the two SwiftUI draws above (only iOS 26 could be checked here); the tab bar fades as it comes and goes. Back lets the keyboard go before the screen moves (UIKit's `resignFirstResponder`, at once, in `Phone.back`, the one Back every arrow calls), so it goes down with Join, never left over Home. No new words, no restyle: all 68 fixtures (70 screens, the intro's pages each) shot by main and by this build on the iPhone 17 simulator and pixel-diffed: 40 identical, the other 30 only in the times that moved between the two runs (bells, countdowns, "ended at") and a text field's blinking caret. Tests: `AppTests.fade`, `.opening`, `.backLetsKeyboardGo` (red first). F11c the review-WARN sweep, server and docs ✅: A19's review (#132) — no contract fixture held a leave that ends a participation; `enrollments/left-past-bell` does (a leave past the bell, its session not swept yet: `endedParticipation: true`), the fixture test asks for both values (red first), BaliCore decodes it and `LeaveTests.answers` reads it as out. F2's review (#157) — ios/README.md's round 1 step 12 names the known case: access taken back in a class's last 15 minutes, turned back on in Settings, then a re-tap, whose window has not begun, so Screen Time can read off until the tap's answer (offline, that window's start, past the bell) or Allow. And C4b's entry names its fixture as F7 renamed it, `homeWaiting`. No app or API change. F11a-1 the merged PRs' review WARNs, part 1 — sign-in and the fade ✅: **another student's sign-in shows nothing of the last one's, at any moment** (#160's review): until the engine's `forgetMe` landed, Home could greet Bea as "Hi, Ana" with Ana's classes — or, Ana once in a class here and in none now, her empty Home in place of Join. Now the last student's `me` goes from what the phone shows at the sign-in itself, and a state the engine sent before it forgot it — the newest is kept on its way — comes without it: the engine counts its forgets (`SyncState.forgets`), the phone the ones it asked for (`Phone.forgets`), so the gate is exact, whatever the stream's timing. **A class code typed goes with who typed it, a look-up or a join on its way too** (Riders-2's review): the code went only once nothing was under way, so the answer landed for the next student — Ana's class and teacher, or "No class has that code" — now cleared at once and the answer dropped (`Phone.signIns`), the join's too, so it never marks a try of theirs done; and the engine adds a join answered after another student's sign-in to no one's `me` (`SyncEngine.join`, santa's round 1: it went into the next student's, and kept them as once in a class). **The screen leaving takes no touch, nor VoiceOver** (#161's review; `Opening.Replaced`): in a test switching screens as `RootView` does, iOS 26 let go of the leaving one at once, so this holds for SwiftUI keeping it (another iOS, or a removal that animates). `backLetsKeyboardGo` looks after each render, past the fade's end (#161's review); `Tokens.init` asks for the email, no default (#159's review); `offersSignOut`'s comment brought up to F8. No visual change, no new words, no server change: all 68 fixtures (70 screens) shot by main and by this build on the iPhone 17 simulator and pixel-diffed — 41 identical, the other 29 only in the times that moved between the two runs (bells, countdowns, "ended at") and a text field's blinking caret. Tests: `AppTests.nothingOfTheLast`, `.joiningSignedOut`, `.leavingTakesNoTouch`, `TokenTests.emailAsked`, `JoinEngineTests.joinedAcrossSignIn` (red first), `.backLetsKeyboardGo`, `MeTests.forgetMe`. F12 (CI) a PR merges only after its Claude Review has passed ✅: the review job runs on drafts too, reviews nothing there and fails ("Draft: the review runs when the PR is marked ready."), and a PR's checks read a check's newest run, so the review `ready_for_review` starts takes the draft run's place — until it has passed, nothing merges, `--auto` or not (#154 merged 3 s after it was marked ready, before its review began). Same check name, so `protect-main` is unchanged; GOTCHAS' draft entry is gone, and a new one says the API keeps a draft's failed run after the review passes (judge a PR by `mergeStateStatus` or `gh pr checks`). A draft now shows a red ✗ for Claude Review until it is marked ready. F1b (#144) a running Bali notices Screen Time access taken back by a marker — ✅ proven on the phone (2026-10-03: revoked with Bali open, protection off on the grid 5 s later; opened with access off, off at once; revoked with Bali in the background, off at once as it opened; the first launch after the update, the marker present, the silent ask 0.008 s, no prompt): the owner's device experiment (2026-10-02, #144) found Family Controls' read approved in a running app through a revocation, its publisher silent, and iOS deleting Bali's whole ManagedSettings record at it — so the first store read after Bali comes back is empty. The marker: a value of no effect (`media.denyExplicitContent = false`) in a store of its own (`bali.marker`, never the shields', which the monitor clears at the bell), written once iOS confirms the authorization, and its flag in the app group's defaults, which a revocation leaves, set once the marker reads back (`Marker`, `PhoneBell.swift`). Judged at every check — each check-in, each return to the front, a launch's first wake — and by every pass (`Enforcer.judged`): denied off at once; the marker there approved at once, a launch's not determined too (#145: no "Checking Screen Time…" wait); gone with its flag set, off at once whatever the read — protection off reported, the Screen Time off screen, its way on iOS's prompt, then a re-tap — and no shield ever put back over it, which iOS would keep without enforcing. No marker yet (a first run, or a build before this): the read judges as before, B5a-2's grace kept there only, and an approved read is proven silently, one `requestAuthorization` in the background — never over not determined, never off on its own — the marker written. One ask of iOS at a time (`Enforcer.asking`: a press during one has its answer, no `authorizationConflict`); given, the bell's window asked for anew and the shields back where the standing calls for them (the re-tap rule unchanged; #167). The monitor reads the marker at a window's end or start: gone, it notes when in the app group and asks iOS for no next wake (a clear still clears), and the app reports it at its next run, past the bell too, the class running when it went (the server's `after_session_end`). Retired: F1's doubt and its `unauthorized` refusal (`ScreenTime.holds`, `ScreenTimeUnauthorized`), F2's not-begun guard, an approved read as proof. Debug readout: `Marker:` (present, MISSING, or none written yet, with the monitor's note) and `Asked iOS:` (each ask's answer and time — the silent check's is the one no press made); `Found:` gone. Tests: `RevokedWhileRunningTests` (rewritten for the marker), `LaunchGraceTests.markerAtLaunch`, `RegisterTests.accessLost`, red first. README round 1 steps 7, 12 and 13 say what the phone check must see. F13 (#167) Screen Time back on in the class the student tapped into returns them where they stood, with no re-tap (the owner's decision, 2026-10-02) — F13a, the server half ✅: `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/protection-on` (additive; `protectionOn`, one more rule set on `changeState`) takes a live participation out of protection off to the state its latest turn there says (A9's `latestTurn`) — focused, or unlocked after an unlock, one recorded while it was off included — recorded as its own `protection_on` event, the protection off kept; refused past the bell, swept or not (A17), for anyone not live (`not_participating`), and `409 protection_not_off` where protection is not off (a re-tap still leaves it); a replay after the student left names no session (A4); late by the phone's order when a protection off it made after it is on record (`superseded`, never applied). A late unlock (A10) is noted `superseded` ahead of `protection_off` now (the Claude Review's blocker), so back on never returns to one that landed while protection was off. The grid's chip goes back to Focused, or Unlocked when it carries an unlock; History's moment is "Screen Time back on" (a neutral shield). With F1b's three Claude Review WARNs (#169), red first: the monitor's note kept until the marker reads back (`Marker.written`), the silent ask once a launch at most (`Enforcer.askedSilently`), and `Marker.noted`'s decision on Linux (`Marker.noting`). Tests: `transitions.test.ts` ("Screen Time back on"), `session-lifecycle.test.ts` (`protection-on`), `races.test.ts` (#167: an unlock, a late unlock, a re-tap, a teacher's end and the sweep, both orders), `grid-state.test.ts`, fixtures `protection-on/*` and `history/every-kind`, `MarkerBookTests`, `RevokedWhileRunningTests.silentCheck` and `.silentCheckBounded`. F13b, the phone — ✅ proven on the phone (2026-10-03: Allow → Focus with no re-tap, one prompt for a double tap; after an Emergency Unlock it stays unlocked): after Turn on Screen Time is answered, and at each check, a phone standing protection off in a class still running by its own clock, its marker there again (never Family Controls' read alone), sends Screen Time back on through the outbox (`Change.protectionOn`, outbox v6) and goes back at once where it stood before protection off — kept with the report's own write (`protectionOffFrom`) until a tap or the next report, so a back on made again returns there too — unlocked after an Emergency Unlock since; not known, unlocked while an Emergency Unlock is still queued there, else focused — so the shields come back offline too, the server's answer reconciling. Made once: the engine records it only out of protection off, so overlapping checks make one. Back on to unlocked, the Unlocked card's reason is still changeable (A20): Screen Time going off or coming back is no turn of the student's. The Screen Time off screen leaves with no tap: its card's second step reads "Bali puts you back in class by itself", the re-tap kept only where Screen Time reads on and that could not be done (a marker that does not read back); "Checking Screen Time…" says "until it's back on". Tests: `BackOnTests` (focused again, still unlocked, made once, unlocked through a late answer, an unlock since, not known over a queued unlock, past the bell or out, a marker not back, a relaunch, refused or late), `SchemaTests.fromV5` and `.offFrom`, `ScreenTests.backOn`, `ProtectionOffScreenTests`, the settle tests over `protection-on`; fixture `historyScreenTime` (History's Screen Time off, an unlock under it, Screen Time back on). README round 1 step 12 says what the phone check must see. F11a-2 the merged PRs' review WARNs, part 2 — Me, Leave, Home and Unlocked ✅: **"hasn't reached your teacher yet" goes once the unlock has** (#129's review): Me says it where the outbox file holds an unlock the engine's queue does not show, and it went only once a hold the queue showed ended, so never where the queue's reads failed throughout; that end still clears it at once, and now, where no hold ever showed, the file itself is asked again at each state whose queue shows none, the words going once it holds none. **Me lets go of a class's Leave at its lesson's bell by itself** (#134's review): `ClassesSection` judges each class's hold once, so its Leave and its line agree, and wakes at the bell (`Screen.bell`), as Home's card does; the router already leaves Me for Session over there, so nothing shows differently. **A leave refused 403 for a reason this build does not know** is said as the route's own no, never the join's words (#134's review). **On Home in no class a refused tap says "Bali couldn't record a tap. Try again, or ask your teacher."**: that Home has no Tap in, and its Try again is beside the words (#160's review; fixture `homeNoClassesTapStuck`). **A pick's hold is its own** (#152's review): kept as the card it was made on and let go as its loop ends, whichever way, so a pick on the next unlock's card is never held back and nothing else resets it (`synced` no longer does). **The engine's leave takes nothing from a `me` forgotten meanwhile**, as its join adds nothing (#164's review). The app's tests ask the router once, `Phone.shown` (#129's review; `screen` private, `tabbed` gone), and `TokenTests.emailEveryWay` checks that every way the phone makes tokens keeps the email, a relaunch reading it back, in place of `emailAsked`'s read of the source (#164's review). Visible change: the one line on Home in no class. Tests: `AppTests.signOutWiring`, `.leaveAtTheBell`, `LeaveTests.answers`, `LeaveEngineTests.leftAcrossSignIn`, `ScreenTests.refusedTapInNoClass` (red first); `AppTests.picksPastTheirCard` (main passes it; red with `synced` no longer resetting the hold and `pick` unchanged, the WARN's case) and `TokenTests.emailEveryWay` (red under a mutation dropping the email on the engine's renewal). F14 (#166) a wait the server has dropped is Home, never Waiting — ✅ proven on the phone (2026-10-03: a tap, then offline through a Start and its end; back online, the regular Home within about 30 s). The owner's goal (2026-10-02): a tap at 7:10 PM still said "Ready — waiting for your teacher" the next evening, and a Start then joined nothing, since the server drops an armed tap at the end of its school day and nothing told the phone (C3a's known gap). Server: `GET /v1/me` gains `armed` (additive; `hasArmedTap`): a tap of the caller's not taken by a Start, not past its `expires_at` by the server's clock, for a teacher of a live class they are in — the Start's own read of its waiting taps (`lockWaitingTaps`), so the two never disagree; false for a teacher. Phone: decision 6's 30 s read ends the wait once `armed` is false (`standing(me)`; nil, an older API's, leaves it) — Home, its in-session card with Tap in where a class of theirs runs (C3c), plain Home otherwise — which also ends C3a's accepted stale screen (a tap taken by a Start whose class ended unheard); a read never starts a wait. Offline, each arming sets the wait's end, the midnight after it in the phone's zone — the server's rule in the school's (`SyncState.waitEnds`), kept in the outbox file for a relaunch (`waitEnds`, a key, no migration) — and the read loop ends the wait at its first wake past it (30 s in the foreground, at once coming back); a wait an earlier build kept has no end, so only a read ends it. No new words or screens; the Debug readout's `Standing:` says the end. Tests: `me.test.ts` (#166: the end of its school day, a Start after it, joined then over, only a Start's own taps), fixtures `me/no-session` (`armed: true`) and `me/armed-expired`, `DroppedWaitTests` (a read saying none, a Start after expiry, a class over unswept, offline at the end, an end not kept, relaunched, the end's rule) and `ReadTests.waitingStays`, red first; santa's round 1: `armed` read before the session (a Start landing between the two reads), red first. README round 1 step 15 says what the phone check must see. F11b the merged PRs' review WARNs, part 3 — History, and the tests' hygiene ✅: **History on screen as the app comes back to the front from the background reads its newest page again**, quietly, as each visit does (#153's review; `Phone.setForeground`) — never from only inactive (Control Center pulled down and up), whose read from the top would take Show earlier's pages from a student reading them (santa's round 1), nor under another screen. **A history read is counted as it starts** (`reads`), the engine started or not, so Show earlier's page landing after a read from the top is never taken for the newest. **The "couldn't update" card keeps its words while its Try again reads, the button saying Reading…**, dimmed (`Retry`'s `reading`): the words stay until a read from the top answers, where they went the moment one started, nothing in their place (fixture `historyRefreshReading`). **A teacher's account reads no history**: the sign-in's read waits for `GET /v1/me` to name a student (`historyDue`), at once where it already does, so the API's 403 is never asked for; History opened there still says the account's words. **A sign-in Bali couldn't check is said once**: "Bali couldn't check your sign-in to update your history. Try again." (fixture `historyRefreshSignIn`). **History's days are drawn once per change of the history, or of the day** (`Phone.historyDays`, #156's review), kept from one visit to the next — anew with each read, so a clock or locale changed meanwhile shows at the next (santa's round 1). **A card is known by its day, its class and how many of that class's cards come after it that day** (`History.Card.id`), so Show earlier's older moment joining it keeps it the card it was, the scroll with it; only a class come back to after another, read from the top, renews its earlier cards that day. Hygiene: `homeTapRefused`'s fixture waits 30 s at most for its outbox, then stops saying so (#155's review); the engine reads the newest tap in a step of its own (`knowLastTap`), never as the queue's read's hidden effect (#155's review); the intro is opened at each of its pages (`IntroView(page:)`) for the scroll-edge check, never paged by widths (#135's review: it checked the pager's slots, and a zero width stopped the suite); every Me fixture is found by its screen (#159's review); the past-bell leave's tap must have joined (`contract-fixtures.test.ts`'s `setup`, #163's review); Me's unsent ask cancels the last (`unsentCheck`, #172's review); `leaveAtTheBell` draws its references first, so only the one window's drawing must beat the bell; and `TokenTests.emailNoDefault` reads `Tokens.init`'s source again, whatever its layout, beside `emailEveryWay` (#172's review). Visible change: History's card while its Try again reads, and its words for a sign-in Bali couldn't check. No server change. ## Go-live features

### In scope for launch (phase noted)

| Feature | Phase | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Core loop: tap→shield offline, armed taps, live grid, unlock always-recorded, refocus, join codes, roster, removal, self-expiry | 1–2 | ✅ built. **Leaving a class (A19 ✅):** never while it is in session — `409 class_in_session`. **Self-expiry to the minute (A15 ✅):** the API sweeps every minute by itself; Railway's cron, every 5 minutes, is its backup. **On the phone (B6a ✅):** the block read over NFC — the code written on it — into the tap, shielded at once offline — Home's **Tap in** (Debug builds until C4), and a tap armed before the Start waiting on its own screen, the truth read every 30 s while Bali is open (C3a, C3b; decision 6, the owner's ruling); an Emergency Unlock before the tap's answer filed under that tap (decision 11) — and again in the class the student stood in when the scan joins no class, the owner's ruling (B6b ✅); over a standing the phone cannot read, Emergency Unlock still takes the shields off, kept until it can be filed (B6b ✅) |
| 30s check-in that verifies shields before claiming them | 3 | rule 3. **API ✅ (A2):** a revoked permission is reported with `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/protection-off`; refocus is refused out of it and an unlock never softens it (the grid mirrors the unlock rule; a refocus is never recorded out of it, so there is none to mirror) — only a re-tap returns to focus. A report that first reaches the server after the bell is recorded with a note, like a late unlock (A2c). **Phone ✅ (B3b-2):** the check-in every 30 s in the foreground, its answer reconciled. **Shields ✅ (B5a):** before each check-in and on coming to the foreground, the shields go back on if iOS lost them, a revoked permission in a session is reported (again whenever the phone stands focused there), and a screen claims only what the check found (`Protection`); a permission read not determined is reported only once it lasts a check-in interval, so a relaunch shows no false protection off (B5a-2); the check runs every 30 s in the foreground offline too, never reports for a session the phone's own clock has ended, and its grace window is measured by uptime, so a clock set forward reports nothing (B5a-3) |
| Shields survive force-quit; bell frees phone via extension | 3 | confirmed on the owner's iPhone (B5b's round 2, 2026-09-27: the shields off 31 s after the bell, the app force-quit). **The bell with the app closed ✅ (B5b):** the window the shields are on registered with iOS as a DeviceActivity schedule, and the monitor extension clearing them at its end, the bell or decision 7's cap, by the phone's clock — never over a session still running nor a file it cannot read; its whole open and read bounded at 2 s, and a wake iOS refuses it shown at the app's next open (B5b-2); the read read only, and 2 s a ceiling (B6c); no wake asking iOS anything of the window that woke it (iOS 18's deadlock), and a backup window two minutes after the bell's, so a lost wake costs two minutes, not until the app is opened (B5b-3). **Relaunch ✅ (B5a):** the standing is kept in the app group, so reopening the app — offline too — never takes the shields off, nor does a standing the file will not give back (B5a-2); a change and the standing it leaves are one write, so a relaunch after a kill never shields over an Emergency Unlock, and the kept form is pinned for later builds (B5a-3) |
| Onboarding: Sign up or sign in → Sign up's 13+ check and intro → sign-up → your name → Screen Time grant → Home | 3 | **The flow (2026-10-07):** a first launch opens on Sign in, "Sign up or sign in", a reinstall's too (2026-10-08); Sign up → the 13+ question, every time → the intro, once per account (seen for the app's run only) → Cognito's sign-up page → Your name for an account with none → Screen Time → Home, its empty state in no class, never a forced Join; Sign in → Cognito's sign-in page at once → the starting mark while Bali's server is asked for the account's 13+ yes (C7-server, 2026-10-08) → passed, on to Your name, Screen Time and Home, an existing student on a new phone or after a reinstall never asked again; a sign-in into an account with no yes there, made around the question on Cognito's own pages or never confirmed, or Bali not reached, gets the question first (the gap's fallback), 13 or older recording the yes; a Sign up records its yes as its sign-in lands; the phone keeps no age note. An update keeps its file and its sign-in, whatever the defaults hold; a phone that reinstalled build 8 or earlier keeps, through the update, the sign-in it took up again there (a delete and reinstall clears it). Required: Continue saves it as Me's card does (`Naming`, `Phone.saveName`, `PATCH /v1/me`; "Saving…" while it runs), each refusal in Me's words under it (a classmate uses the name, Bali can't use it, blank from the keyboard's return, couldn't reach Bali, Continue again its replay), and Sign out, Me's (`SignOutButton`, dimmed while a save runs), is the only other way on; set, the router moves on. A teacher's account, or a role this build does not know, never gets it. Known only from a read: a sign-in made this run, or one the 13+ check let through, holds the starting screen until the first read answers or fails, a read that failed before it forgotten (`Phone.forgetMe`, the router's `signedInThisRun`), so no screen flashes before Your name (the owner's ruling on the review's warn 1); a failed read, or a sign-in kept from the last run, lets the screens go on, and the read that names none shows it. Offline after that read, a nameless student stays on Your name until the network is back or the app is reopened (warn 2, accepted by the owner). No backend change; dev's pool names a new account after its Cognito username, so only fixtures and tests show it there. A wrong account signs out from Me: Join's Sign out is gone (`Phone.offersSignOut`). Known limit: the words key on the student last listed in a class on this phone (`inClass`, #143), so on a phone shared after another student was listed, one removed from their last class reads "not in a class yet". An open grid shows it at its next 15 s snapshot (A9). **Screen ✅ (C6b):** Me — the name edited in place, every refusal said with Save to try again |

| End-of-session recap card (portal) | 4 | **R4 ✅:** the class page shows its last session's report (R2) once the server has marked it over — at the End's answer, the grid's end event or snapshot, or on a fresh visit (R3's newest row) — until a new session starts; who joined, the class's focus and silent minutes, every unlock and protection off; aggregates only, never rankings. At the bell the ended grid stays under it until the next Start (R5) |
| Reports: class focus minutes + unlock list; aggregates only, never rankings | 4 | `armed_tap_skipped` names a student who did NOT join — never count it as a join. An unlock or protection off noted `after_session_end` reached the server after the end, and its time is clamped to the scheduled window, so after an early end it can fall past `ended_at` — never count time beyond it. An unlock noted `superseded` changed no state — the student's own refocus or tap came after it — so never end focus time at it, after the end included. One kept unattached (`tap_armed`, `unknown_tap`, like `unknown_session` and `not_enrolled`) is in no class; if its tap files it later, the filed one names it (`unattached_event_id`) — count one, never both. An unlock the phone's order applied (A12) can carry a time before the return it followed — order a student's own unlock and return as the engine did (by their `order_install` / `order_seq` when both carry one from the same install), never by the times alone. A return noted `superseded` (A13) changed no state either — the unlock after it stands — so never start focus time at it; nor at a tap noted so because a later tap of the student's into another class went ahead of it (A14), which joined nothing — never count it as a join. **R1 ✅:** `sessionReport` (`@bali/shared`) counts by these rules from the stored events, read in the engine's order (`seq`); silence is counted apart from focus (`silentMinutes`), and the minutes before a phone is declared silent still count as focus (the owner's ruling, 2026-10-04). **R2 ✅:** `GET /v1/classes/{id}/reports/sessions/{sessionId}` serves it to the class's own teacher — names now, whole minutes each rounded from its exact figure, a session not over yet answered with `ended: false`; a note this build doesn't know moves no one and reads as none. **R3 ✅:** `GET /v1/classes/{id}/reports/sessions` lists the class's sessions newest first, 20 a page, each with R2's totals counted on read — how many joined, focus minutes (total and average), silent minutes, how many unlocks and protection offs — so a row never disagrees with its report. **R5 ✅:** the portal's reports page, "Reports →" on the class page: the class's sessions marked over, newest first, 20 a page with Show earlier, each row R3's figures and opening its recap (R4's card, fed by R2) |

### Open product decisions (owner to confirm; lean in parentheses)

Decisions 6–12 as they read on 2026-10-13 (1–5 are in `docs/PLAN.md`):

6. How a waiting (armed) phone learns the teacher pressed Start — student
   phones get no live feed, so nothing tells it (lean: poll `GET /v1/me` while
   the app is open and on resume; push rides the fast-follow push work).
   **Decided 2026-09-29 (the owner, on the conductor's recommendation) —
   built (C3a ✅):** the load explained — about one request a second per
   waiting class, never above the in-class check-in's peak — and the
   trade-off: behind another app, the Start is found only at the next return
   to the front. While the phone waits, it reads
   `GET /v1/me` every 30 s in the foreground only — the check-in's cadence —
   and at every return to the front; push notifications are a later upgrade.
   *Since 2026-10-06:* a visible "class started" alert rings the converted students
   (steps N1–N6); the 30 s read stays, since no code assumes the alert arrived.
7. The default end for a tap made with no signal — **decided 2026-09-24:**
   shield at once; if the phone never reaches the server, the shields come off
   on their own after **50 minutes** (the owner's length; iOS can't schedule
   under 15). The real end time replaces the cap as soon as the phone reaches
   the server; Emergency Unlock works throughout. B5a shields to the cap
   while the app runs; B5b schedules it for a closed app.
8. Policing an edited display name — **decided 2026-09-24:** unique within
   each class; a name a classmate in any shared class already uses is refused,
   ignoring case. A8 ✅ implements it.
9. macOS CI for the iOS build — **decided 2026-09-24:** GitHub-hosted macOS
   runners, only on PRs that touch `ios/` (free while the repo is public).
   Revisit when the owner makes the repo private: minutes then count (macOS at
   10×), so the job moves to the owner's Mac as a self-hosted runner (safe only
   on a private repo), and a personal account needs GitHub Pro to keep branch
   rulesets enforced there.
10. A protection-off that first reaches the server after the bell —
    **decided 2026-09-24:** recorded with a note, like a late unlock, so the
    history says why the phone went quiet (a `409` → `200` correction; A2c ✅).
11. An emergency unlock made while the phone's own tap is still unanswered —
    what it is sent to. **Decided 2026-09-24 — built (A11 ✅); B6/C5
    use it:** it is filed under its tap. The phone sends it with the tap's
    `event_id` (`POST /v1/taps/{eventId}/unlock`, additive), and the server
    records it in whatever session that tap landed in — late or noted, as
    today — or as an unattached record with a note when the tap was only
    armed or never arrived; never lost. On the phone, as the lean had it: the
    unlock is queued behind its tap, acted on at once, and guards whatever
    session the tap's answer names. (Naming the session the phone was in
    before the tap is wrong once the tap switches it.) B6a ✅ sends it:
    `SyncEngine.emergencyUnlock` files it under a tap not yet answered,
    and it guards every session until that answer names one. **The
    owner's ruling (2026-09-25), built in B6b ✅:** a scan that joins no
    class — armed, or refused — has the unlock filed again in the class
    the student stood in: one press, two records.
12. Extending a session already past its bell by the server's clock, before
    the sweep has ended it (A16). **Decided 2026-09-30 (the owner) — built
    (A16 ✅): refused.** Every phone clears its shields at the bell by its
    own clock (decision 6), a late extend would show those phones green on
    the grid while unlocked, and whether it took would depend on whether the
    sweep had run yet. So it gets a swept session's answer, `409
    session_not_running`, and the teacher starts a new session: a Start past
    the bell ends the old one itself, as the sweep would (A18 ✅, the owner's
    ruling, 2026-09-30; until then a Start answered with the old one, up to
    the sweep's minute). Phase 2 had allowed it, with a fresh window from the
    press.

## How to update this file (every session that changes code)

Its first rule as it read until 2026-10-13:

- Flip statuses, refresh **Now** (a short handoff: production, what's in flight, the critical
  path, who does what; history goes in the step lines or `docs/DECISIONS.md`), and re-date the
  header line.
